# Code-quality rules audit

**Scope and evidence.** This is a complete catalog of the shared Engineering code-quality policy in [`jdeurt/agents`](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents), plus the repository’s own compiler and coding constraints. The audited default-branch baseline is [`50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2`](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/commit/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2). The effective inventory below describes that baseline **after removing only ESLint `sort-imports`**. The commit containing this report identifies the removal revision; embedding that commit’s own hash would be self-referential. The report is an assessment, not authorization to remove other rules.

Repository identity was verified through GitHub’s repository metadata (`jdeurt/agents`, “Skills and plugins I like.”, default branch `main`) and the source/build links below. Work used a freshly fetched isolated clone at `/Users/juan/Documents/ChatGPT/DX/agents-rule-audit`, branch `chore/remove-import-sorting`. Existing checkouts and installed plugin caches were preserved. The effective audit agent was verified as **`gpt-6-astra`, high reasoning effort** from its current turn record. Audit started October 20, 2026 (America/Los_Angeles).

Included: all **48 numbered written style rules**, **35 adoption/review/repository workflow directives**, **1,156 distinct enabled lint IDs**, **21 formatting controls** (20 exposed style options plus TOML support), and **34 compiler controls** (25 explicit options plus nine inherited strict-family options). Distinct IDs remain separate even when they overlap. One lint ID can have several effective configurations; all such variants appear in its applicability cell. This is a count of policy entries, not independent benefits or rules simultaneously running on one file.

Excluded from the quality catalog: unrelated business/vendor/product skills, standalone migration/refactoring tool playbooks, publication/distribution mechanics, and the complete English-writing handbooks. The code-review obligations to review changed prose and use appropriate writing guidance are included. Imported skill `.import` markers do not make every upstream instruction part of this shared policy. Execution-only Prettier controls are disclosed separately. Compiler settings are cataloged explicitly because they constrain this repository’s checked source; compiler diagnostic numbers and every internal formatter printer branch are not separate policy rules.

The baseline tracks 34 TypeScript files and one `.mjs` file, with no tracked JSX/TSX source. React rules are nevertheless included because the canonical **bundled policy** enables them for future/adopting JSX/TSX projects. The exported `createConfig()` helper defaults to untyped mode; the root default export explicitly uses typed mode. Both are inventoried.

The default typed family has **1,155** distinct active IDs across its extensions. Untyped mode adds one distinct core ID, `no-unused-vars`, giving the audited union of **1,156**. There are **1157 distinct active (rule ID, normalized configuration) variants**, **363 off-only keys**, **22 IDs enabled in some profiles and disabled in others**, and **one removed rule**, recorded outside active totals. Off-only keys include compatibility disables for plugins that are not loaded; they are not 363 formerly enforced rules.

**Improves code quality/maintainability** means a concrete mechanism improves correctness, security, resource ownership, readability, or reviewable contracts. **Uncertain / could improve quality** means a plausible benefit depends on workload, architecture, false-positive behavior, or a subjective readability tradeoff. **Convinced it offers little value** covers cosmetic preferences, redundancy, or currently ineffective configuration with no material independent benefit. These are engineering judgments, not claims that a recommended preset proves value. A useful rule can still report a false positive; no quantitative defect-reduction claim is made.

## Important findings

- Correctness, type/absence checks, async ownership, resource cleanup, protocol contracts, and accessible interface semantics account for many useful rules. Their value comes from the failure they prevent, not the preset label.
- Many layout and spelling rules duplicate the formatter. Thresholds, blanket documentation, eager memoization, and broad performance advice need context; their costs are recorded individually.
- `max-len` is explicitly written as 120 early in the source but is **off in every final profile** because the later Prettier compatibility preset disables it. The active `printWidth: 120` is a soft formatting target, not a hard line-length gate. Written 1B also allows clarity exceptions.
- The skill summary says to separate class members, but the actual override and written 1C require spacing around **methods**, permit adjacent fields, and keep overloads together. This report records the actual configuration and flags the broader summary wording; the task did not authorize editing it.
- Declaration-prefix placement can pull against `unicorn/no-declarations-before-early-exit`. `@typescript-eslint/non-nullable-type-assertion-style` prefers notation also constrained by `no-non-null-assertion`. CommonJS source parsing coexists with `unicorn/prefer-module`. These interacting constraints remain unchanged.
- React Doctor is enabled only for `.jsx`/`.tsx`, including its generic security and graphics checks. Package/version labels in its metadata are **not generally automatic project detection**: without explicit capability settings, positive requirements default to satisfied. Real import, path, JSX-runtime, and Ink-version gates are disclosed below.
- `react-doctor/forbid-elements` and `react-doctor/forbid-dom-props` are enabled IDs but have no forbidden lists in the present configuration. They currently enforce nothing. Other rules can also find no applicable constructs in a particular project.

## Import sorting: exact removal

| Removed rule                                                      | Former applicability and behavior                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Reason and retained protections                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [sort-imports](https://eslint.org/docs/latest/rules/sort-imports) | Warning on all eight extensions in both modes. Default syntax-group order: none, all, multiple, single; then case-sensitive first binding/alias order. Named members are also sorted. ignoreCase=false, ignoreDeclarationSort=false, ignoreMemberSort=false, allowSeparatedGroups=false. This is not simply alphabetical source-path sorting. | Removed at Juan’s explicit request to stop ordering-only churn. No other policy was removed. Import-block spacing, declaration placement, consolidation, duplicate protection, type-only import policy, and side-effect handling remain. |

The source change is exactly:

```diff
-        "sort-imports": "warn",
```

No matching written alphabetical-import instruction exists in the audited guide or skill. Written **1C** (structural spacing), **2H** (explicit supported/consolidated dependencies), `no-duplicate-imports`, and `@stylistic/padding-line-between-statements` remain. `unicorn/no-array-sort` concerns mutation by array sorting and is unrelated. The remaining word “imports” in tooling includes imported plugin content and must not be confused with JavaScript import ordering.

## How the effective configuration was expanded

Bun 1.4.3 installed the frozen lockfile with lifecycle scripts disabled. ESLint 10.13.0’s `ESLint.calculateConfigForFile()` was called on a synthetic file for each extension, for `createConfig({typed:true})` and `createConfig({typed:false})`, using `overrideConfigFile:true`. This exercises ESLint’s real flat-config merge, including preset expansion, override order, default option normalization and disabled replacements. The report does not infer enabled rules from package README lists. No lint file was added to the repository.

| Package                    | Resolved version |
| -------------------------- | ---------------- |
| eslint                     | 10.13.0          |
| @eslint/js                 | 10.0.1           |
| typescript                 | 5.9.3            |
| typescript-eslint          | 8.71.2           |
| @stylistic/eslint-plugin   | 5.10.0           |
| eslint-plugin-unicorn      | 78.0.0           |
| eslint-config-prettier     | 10.1.8           |
| eslint-plugin-prettier     | 5.5.7            |
| eslint-plugin-jsdoc        | 65.3.15          |
| eslint-plugin-react-hooks  | 7.1.1            |
| eslint-plugin-react-doctor | 0.9.18           |
| oxlint-plugin-react-doctor | 0.9.18           |
| prettier                   | 3.9.9            |
| prettier-plugin-toml       | 3.0.2            |
| jiti                       | 2.7.0            |

Layer order: JavaScript recommended → Unicorn recommended → typed strict/stylistic or untyped strict/stylistic TypeScript presets → CommonJS source type → shared limits and overrides → TypeScript replacements → typed unsafe-assertion rule → JSX parser/Hooks/React Doctor → typed project service → Prettier compatibility → nonconflicting Stylistic recommended rules → explicit Prettier/curly/nested-ternary restoration → seven JSDoc rules → custom declaration placement and spacing. The late restoration is why “eslint-config-prettier is enabled” does not mean all stylistic rules are disabled.

| Profile code | Extensions      | Mode                  | Active rules per file |
| ------------ | --------------- | --------------------- | --------------------- |
| TJ           | .js, .mjs, .cjs | typed project service | 564                   |
| TX           | .jsx            | typed project service | 1147                  |
| TT           | .ts, .mts, .cts | typed project service | 551                   |
| TY           | .tsx            | typed project service | 1134                  |
| UJ           | .js, .mjs, .cjs | untyped               | 474                   |
| UX           | .jsx            | untyped               | 1057                  |
| UT           | .ts, .mts, .cts | untyped               | 500                   |
| UY           | .tsx            | untyped               | 1083                  |

Each grouped extension has an identical rule dictionary, verified by equality comparison. **`.cjs` and `.cts` use `sourceType: commonjs`; all others use `module`**, even inside a grouped row. Typed profiles use `projectService:true` with the project root; JSX/TSX enables JSX parsing. In untyped mode only TypeScript extensions receive TypeScript presets. In typed mode many TypeScript plugin rules also apply to JavaScript. Global read-only names are `console`, `Bun`, `process`, and `Buffer`. Global ignores: `dist/**`, `node_modules/**`, `.marketplace-build/**`, `.marketplace-verify/**`.

In every lint row, **S** identifies the exact enabled file/mode set and **O** identifies the normalized option array following severity. Multiple entries mean different effective settings; none are discarded. `O000 = []` means no configured option arguments, not that the implementation has no defaults. Locked implementations supply remaining defaults. The evidence extraction reads each rule’s actual `meta.docs.description`, default options, schema and messages; React Doctor’s original title/recommendation was also read for every active ID because the ESLint wrapper’s title alone is terse. Selected ambiguous behaviors were checked in implementation source.

### React Doctor applicability details

All its active rows inherit the JSX/TSX profile restriction. Additional row annotations use these meanings:

- **N**: the implementation’s `test-noise` gate skips recognized test/fixture/tooling paths, unless tagged `migration-hint`. Exact path lists are in the gate appendix.
- **J**: `react-jsx-only` suppresses these visitors for recognized non-React JSX runtimes/markers, while explicit React-runtime evidence wins.
- **I**: the file must import/require/re-export one of the listed package prefixes; exact package or package subpath matches. Dynamic literal imports are included.
- **Ink ≥**: the installed Ink version is actually checked.
- **target** and **disabledWhen**: declared capability labels. With current settings `{ "react-doctor": { "portedRuleMode": "curated" } }` and **no capabilities array**, target requirements are assumed satisfied and disabledWhen labels do not suppress the rule. These are not claims that the target package is installed.

Rule-specific visitors still require their relevant syntax/API. All active raw rules have `framework: global`; their individual package applicability is expressed by the gates and API named by the rule. Tags such as `three`, `r3f`, `webgl`, and `server-action` describe the relevant constructs. Driver-only `diffScope` and `matchByOccurrence` metadata do not change this ESLint profile’s file selection. The two empty-list no-ops remain counted as enabled IDs.

Batching remains conditional on preserved rendering semantics.         |

### Adoption, review and repository directives

P/R/G labels are audit-local inventory keys for unnumbered source directives, not invented canonical rule IDs. A key groups one operational obligation and its implementation details; repeated style principles remain in their numbered source entries.

| Audit key / directive / source                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Applicability                                  | Concern         | Independent assessment                                                                                                                                                   |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- | --------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| [P01. Honor explicit project rules before shared policy; observed conventions fill only gaps](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/plugins/engineering/skills/formatting-linting/SKILL.md)                                             | All adoption and review                        | Maintainability | Keeps agents from replacing deliberate project contracts with generic preferences and gives reviewers an explicit authority for decisions.                               |
| [P02. Inspect language, package manager, existing configuration, source roots, and generated output](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/plugins/engineering/skills/formatting-linting/SKILL.md)                                      | Before policy adoption or edits                | Maintainability | Establishes the actual tool and file boundaries before applying rules, preventing unsupported configuration and generated-file churn.                                    |
| [P03. Use the project's package manager; prefer Bun for a new Bun project](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/plugins/engineering/skills/formatting-linting/SKILL.md)                                                                | Package operations                             | Reproducibility | Preserves lockfile and dependency resolution conventions so reviewers can reproduce the agent's environment.                                                             |
| [P04. Preserve requested policy and fix violations without weakening rules](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/plugins/engineering/skills/formatting-linting/SKILL.md)                                                               | All changes                                    | Governance      | Prevents agents from hiding defects by disabling checks and keeps review criteria stable across the change.                                                              |
| [P05. Use native language constructs for the shared style criteria](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/plugins/engineering/skills/formatting-linting/SKILL.md)                                                                       | Cross-language coding and review               | Maintainability | Transfers the underlying contract without forcing TypeScript-specific syntax or idioms onto another language.                                                            |
| [P06. Read and copy the three policy files together, preserving filenames and relative imports](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/plugins/engineering/skills/formatting-linting/SKILL.md)                                           | JavaScript/TypeScript adoption                 | Reproducibility | Keeps configuration, local rules, and formatter options aligned so adoption does not silently omit part of the policy.                                                   |

| [P08. | [P09. Use compatible pinned dependencies, retain jiti and needed runtime types, and verify presets/APIs on upgrades](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/plugins/engineering/skills/formatting-linting/SKILL.md)                      | Policy dependency setup and upgrades           | Reproducibility | Prevents version drift from silently changing enabled rules or breaking configuration loading.                                                                           |

Cover every owned JS/TS file with strict, allowJs, checkJs, and noEmit type checking](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/plugins/engineering/skills/formatting-linting/SKILL.md)                                               | Typed project setup                            | Type safety     | Checks real source boundaries and JavaScript contracts instead of obtaining a clean result by excluding difficult files.                                                 |

| [P17. | [R03. Read all relevant style criteria and inspect behavior/ownership before proposing structural changes](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/plugins/engineering/skills/code-review/SKILL.md)                                       | Code review                                    | Maintainability | Prevents superficial refactors from overriding resource or API contracts that determine whether the change is valid.                                                     |

Direct task instructions govern test level and location. |

### Formatting controls

Options are from Prettier 3.9.9’s actual support metadata, combined with root configuration. The command formats owned supported files; this repository excludes all `plugins/` content. A syntax-specific option is dormant when that syntax is absent. Prettier’s intrinsic printer behavior is represented by the `prettier/prettier` policy row, not thousands of inferred micro-rules.

| Option / source documentation                                                                                                                               | Effective value and applicability                                   | Concern         | Independent assessment                                                                                                |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [htmlWhitespaceSensitivity](https://prettier.io/docs/options#html-whitespace-sensitivity)                                                                   | `"css"` (inherited default); HTML/Vue/Angular/Handlebars whitespace | Correctness     | Respecting CSS-sensitive whitespace avoids formatting changes that alter visible inline spacing.                      |
| [endOfLine](https://prettier.io/docs/options#end-of-line)                                                                                                   | `"lf"` (explicit); All formatted text                               | Maintainability | Consistent LF endings avoid whole-file diffs and platform-dependent line-ending churn.                                |
| [F21. Enable the TOML formatter plugin only for *.toml](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/prettier.config.mjs) | Owned TOML formatted by Prettier                                    | Correctness     | Uses a TOML-aware parser/printer so configuration formatting respects TOML syntax rather than JavaScript assumptions. |

### Compiler controls

Source: [`tsconfig.json`](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/tsconfig.json). Applies to the repository TypeScript program (`include: ["**/*"]` with dependencies/build outputs excluded) and checked JavaScript. Nine strict-family defaults were expanded with TypeScript 5.9.3’s own `optionDeclarations` / `getStrictOptionValue`, not assumed from a future documentation version. `strict` is an umbrella entry; its children are distinct settings but not additive independent benefits. These compiler controls are separate from the bundled ESLint helper’s typed/untyped choice.

| Setting / official documentation                                                                                  | Effective value and origin     | Concern               | Independent assessment                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------ | --------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [noEmit](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/noEmit.html)                                                     | `true` — explicit              | Build integrity       | Type checking produces no competing JavaScript output, matching direct Bun execution and preventing stale generated files from entering review.                                                            |
| [allowJs](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/allowJs.html)                                                   | `true` — explicit              | Coverage              | Includes owned JavaScript in the same dependency graph, so agents cannot bypass checking merely by changing a file extension.                                                                              |
| [checkJs](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/checkJs.html)                                                   | `true` — explicit              | Type safety           | Reports JavaScript type errors using inferred and documented contracts, exposing invalid calls and accesses to reviewers before execution.                                                                 |
| [isolatedModules](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/isolatedModules.html)                                   | `true` — explicit              | Build correctness     | Rejects constructs that need whole-program transformation, matching Bun's per-file transpilation and avoiding code that type-checks but cannot be transformed safely.                                      |
| [verbatimModuleSyntax](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/verbatimModuleSyntax.html)                         | `true` — explicit              | Module correctness    | Makes type-only imports explicit and preserves value-import intent, helping agents and reviewers reason about runtime dependencies and side effects.                                                       |
| [strict](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/strict.html)                                                     | `true` — explicit              | Type safety           | Enables the compiler's strict family together so missing annotations, nullability, and incompatible calls cannot silently fall back to permissive defaults. Child checks are individually cataloged below. |
| [noImplicitAny](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/noImplicitAny.html)                                       | `true` — inherited from strict | Type safety           | Rejects accidental any inference at untyped boundaries, requiring agents to state or infer a reviewable contract instead of losing checking downstream.                                                    |
| [strictNullChecks](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/strictNullChecks.html)                                 | `true` — inherited from strict | Correctness           | Keeps absence distinct from a present value, forcing a check before operations that would otherwise fail on null or undefined.                                                                             |
| [strictFunctionTypes](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/strictFunctionTypes.html)                           | `true` — inherited from strict | Type safety           | Rejects unsafe function-parameter assignments that can pass a broader input than a callback handles. Method bivariance remains a known compiler exception.                                                 |
| [strictBindCallApply](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/strictBindCallApply.html)                           | `true` — inherited from strict | Correctness           | Checks receiver-adjacent invocation arguments against the function signature, catching invalid bind/call/apply combinations hidden by generic invocation syntax.                                           |
| [strictPropertyInitialization](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/strictPropertyInitialization.html)         | `true` — inherited from strict | Correctness           | Requires class fields to be initialized or explicitly admit absence, making construction guarantees visible before consumers access state.                                                                 |
| [strictBuiltinIteratorReturn](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/strictBuiltinIteratorReturn.html)           | `true` — inherited from strict | Correctness           | Prevents iterator completion values from becoming any, so agents must distinguish a yielded item from the completed iterator's undefined result.                                                           |
| [noImplicitThis](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/noImplicitThis.html)                                     | `true` — inherited from strict | Type safety           | Rejects an untyped receiver, exposing callbacks and methods whose this binding cannot support the assumed operations.                                                                                      |
| [useUnknownInCatchVariables](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/useUnknownInCatchVariables.html)             | `true` — inherited from strict | Correctness           | Requires checking thrown values before reading Error properties, matching JavaScript's ability to throw strings, objects, or other values.                                                                 |
| [noUnusedLocals](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/noUnusedLocals.html)                                     | `true` — explicit              | Maintainability       | Flags unused local bindings that can indicate incomplete wiring or obsolete work, while keeping reviewers focused on values that affect the implementation.                                                |
| [exactOptionalPropertyTypes](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/exactOptionalPropertyTypes.html)             | `true` — explicit              | Type safety           | Distinguishes an absent property from a property explicitly set to undefined, preserving contracts that use property presence during merging or serialization.                                             |
| [noImplicitReturns](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/noImplicitReturns.html)                               | `true` — explicit              | Correctness           | Finds paths that omit a result while sibling paths return, making accidental partial function contracts visible before callers consume undefined.                                                          |
| [noFallthroughCasesInSwitch](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/noFallthroughCasesInSwitch.html)             | `true` — explicit              | Correctness           | Flags nonempty switch branches that flow into another case, preventing accidental execution of the next branch's behavior.                                                                                 |
| [noUncheckedIndexedAccess](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/noUncheckedIndexedAccess.html)                 | `true` — explicit              | Correctness           | Adds possible undefined to indexed lookups, requiring agents to handle missing keys or out-of-range values before use.                                                                                     |
| [noImplicitOverride](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/noImplicitOverride.html)                             | `true` — explicit              | Maintainability       | Requires an explicit override marker so base-class renames or signature changes cannot silently leave a supposedly overridden method disconnected.                                                         |
| [moduleResolution](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/moduleResolution.html)                                 | `"Bundler"` — explicit         | Runtime compatibility | Bundler resolution aligns type lookup with Bun-style module loading, reducing mismatches caused by applying Node's different extension-resolution rules.                                                   |
| [types](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/types.html)                                                       | `["bun"]` — explicit           | Type safety           | Restricts ambient package types to Bun, exposing the intended runtime APIs without unrelated installed type packages silently adding globals.                                                              |
| [allowImportingTsExtensions](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/allowImportingTsExtensions.html)             | `true` — explicit              | Module clarity        | Permits explicit .ts dependencies used by direct Bun execution, letting agents name the real source module without inventing a nonexistent compiled file.                                                  |
| [noUncheckedSideEffectImports](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/noUncheckedSideEffectImports.html)         | `true` — explicit              | Module correctness    | Checks unresolved side-effect imports that could otherwise silently escape type checking, exposing misspelled initialization dependencies.                                                                 |
| [allowUnusedLabels](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/allowUnusedLabels.html)                               | `false` — explicit             | Correctness           | Rejecting unused labels catches accidental label syntax and obsolete control-flow names that can mislead a reviewer about a branch's role.                                                                 |
| [allowUnreachableCode](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/allowUnreachableCode.html)                         | `false` — explicit             | Correctness           | Rejecting syntactically unreachable code exposes mistaken return/throw placement and removes apparent behavior that can never execute.                                                                     |
| [forceConsistentCasingInFileNames](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/forceConsistentCasingInFileNames.html) | `true` — explicit              | Portability           | Checks import filename casing so source accepted on a case-insensitive machine does not fail on a case-sensitive runner.                                                                                   |

### react-hooks lint rules

11 entries. Configuration source: [`eslint.config.ts`](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/eslint.config.ts); rule links point to primary documentation supplied by the locked implementation.

| Rule ID / documentation                                                                                            | Effective applicability, severity, options | Concern     | Independent assessment                                                                                                       |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------ | ----------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [react-hooks/error-boundaries](https://react.dev/reference/eslint-plugin-react-hooks/lints/error-boundaries)       | [S09](#s09) **error** [O000](#o000)        | Correctness | Catches try/catch patterns that cannot catch child rendering errors and directs error ownership to a working boundary.       |
| [react-hooks/exhaustive-deps](https://github.com/facebook/react/issues/14920)                                      | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)         | Correctness | Complete dependencies prevent stale closures and make effect synchronization inputs visible during review.                   |
| [react-hooks/globals](https://react.dev/reference/eslint-plugin-react-hooks/lints/globals)                         | [S09](#s09) **error** [O000](#o000)        | Correctness | Keeps render retries from writing shared global state, making output depend on explicit inputs.                              |
| [react-hooks/immutability](https://react.dev/reference/eslint-plugin-react-hooks/lints/immutability)               | [S09](#s09) **error** [O000](#o000)        | Correctness | Prevents mutation of React snapshots, preserving predictable rerenders and earlier state observations.                       |
| [react-hooks/purity](https://react.dev/reference/eslint-plugin-react-hooks/lints/purity)                           | [S09](#s09) **error** [O000](#o000)        | Correctness | Finds known impure render operations that produce different results when React repeats evaluation.                           |
| [react-hooks/refs](https://react.dev/reference/eslint-plugin-react-hooks/lints/refs)                               | [S09](#s09) **error** [O000](#o000)        | Correctness | Restricts render-time mutable ref access that can diverge across retries and concurrent rendering.                           |
| [react-hooks/rules-of-hooks](https://react.dev/reference/rules/rules-of-hooks)                                     | [S09](#s09) **error** [O000](#o000)        | Correctness | Stable hook call order preserves state association across renders and prevents conditional-hook runtime failures.            |
| [react-hooks/set-state-in-render](https://react.dev/reference/eslint-plugin-react-hooks/lints/set-state-in-render) | [S09](#s09) **error** [O000](#o000)        | Correctness | Catches state updates that can create render loops, preserving the separation between calculating UI and triggering changes. |
| [react-hooks/static-components](https://react.dev/reference/eslint-plugin-react-hooks/lints/static-components)     | [S09](#s09) **error** [O000](#o000)        | Correctness | Stable component identity avoids unintended remounts and state loss when a parent renders again.                             |
| [react-hooks/use-memo](https://react.dev/reference/eslint-plugin-react-hooks/lints/use-memo)                       | [S09](#s09) **error** [O000](#o000)        | Correctness | Checks memoization contracts so a cache does not silently omit computation or depend on invalid usage.                       |
| [react-hooks/void-use-memo](https://react.dev/reference/eslint-plugin-react-hooks/lints/void-use-memo)             | [S09](#s09) **error** [O000](#o000)        | Correctness | Finds memo callbacks or consumers that discard the intended value, exposing mistaken useMemo side effects.                   |

### Adoption, review and repository directives

P/R/G labels are audit-local inventory keys for unnumbered source directives, not invented canonical rule IDs. A key groups one operational obligation and its implementation details; repeated style principles remain in their numbered source entries.

| Audit key / directive / source                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Applicability                   | Concern         | Independent assessment                                                                                                                                    |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------- | --------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [P13. Run lint with zero warnings, formatter checks, and tsc --noEmit](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/plugins/engineering/skills/formatting-linting/SKILL.md)                                  | Verification                    | Governance      | Uniform gates expose unresolved findings, but making every style warning blocking can create disproportionate noise and workarounds.                      |
| [R01. Review every completed logical change separately](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/plugins/engineering/skills/code-review/SKILL.md)                                                        | Completed features and fixes    | Review process  | Distinct reviews can keep scope understandable, but rigidly splitting tightly coupled changes can repeat work and hide their combined contract.           |
| [R06. Scan Type-1, Type-2, and Type-3 duplicates across appropriate source, then filter to changed-code pairs](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/plugins/engineering/skills/code-review/SKILL.md) | Added or changed code           | Maintainability | Whole-source comparison finds copies beyond the diff, but exhaustive scanning on tiny edits can cost more than the duplication risk warrants.             |
| [G01. Use Bun TypeScript and direct TypeScript execution without adding JavaScript compilation](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/AGENTS.md)                                                      | This repository's authored code | Tooling         | One runtime path simplifies maintenance here, but banning a compilation step is an environment choice rather than a general quality improvement.          |
| [G05. Require lint, types, formatting, import validation, build consistency, and tests before commit](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/AGENTS.md)                                                | Repository contribution checks  | Review process  | Broad verification catches packaging regressions, but running every check locally for every edit is costly. This task explicitly moves full suites to CI. |

### Formatting controls

Options are from Prettier 3.9.9’s actual support metadata, combined with root configuration. The command formats owned supported files; this repository excludes all `plugins/` content. A syntax-specific option is dormant when that syntax is absent. Prettier’s intrinsic printer behavior is represented by the `prettier/prettier` policy row, not thousands of inferred micro-rules.

| Option / source documentation                                                               | Effective value and applicability                                                           | Concern         | Independent assessment                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [objectWrap](https://prettier.io/docs/options#object-wrap)                                  | `"preserve"` (inherited default); Supported object-literal printers                         | Readability     | Preserving deliberate multiline objects can retain useful grouping, but existing layout may be accidental and can perpetuate unnecessary vertical space. |
| [semi](https://prettier.io/docs/options#semicolons)                                         | `true` (explicit); JavaScript/TypeScript statements                                         | Readability     | Visible terminators can make statement boundaries explicit, but Prettier's semicolon-free mode also guards automatic-semicolon-insertion hazards.        |
| [quoteProps](https://prettier.io/docs/options#quote-props)                                  | `"as-needed"` (inherited default); JavaScript/TypeScript object keys                        | Readability     | Quoting only required property names reduces punctuation, but consistently quoting a data-shaped object can better convey its role.                      |
| [trailingComma](https://prettier.io/docs/options#trailing-commas)                           | `"es5"` (explicit); Supported multiline lists; ES5 positions, not function parameters/calls | Maintainability | Trailing commas can keep item-addition diffs local, but ES5-only placement is a compatibility/style choice rather than a universal improvement.          |
| [singleAttributePerLine](https://prettier.io/docs/options#single-attribute-per-line)        | `false` (inherited default); HTML/Vue/JSX attributes                                        | Readability     | Allowing compact short elements avoids excessive vertical space, but one-attribute lines can be easier to review for complex elements.                   |
| [proseWrap](https://prettier.io/docs/options#prose-wrap)                                    | `"preserve"` (inherited default); Markdown prose                                            | Maintainability | Preserving source prose wrapping avoids unrelated churn, but it can retain lines that are awkward to review in narrow views.                             |
| [printWidth](https://prettier.io/docs/options#print-width)                                  | `120` (explicit); All supported printers, as a soft target                                  | Readability     | A 120-column target can improve scanning in review panes, but long literals and expressions sometimes need exceptions and it is not a hard limit.        |
| [embeddedLanguageFormatting](https://prettier.io/docs/options#embedded-language-formatting) | `"auto"` (inherited default); Recognized embedded code in supported host syntaxes           | Maintainability | Formatting recognized embedded code improves internal readability, but embedded syntax and intentional string layout need semantic review.               |

### Compiler controls

Source: [`tsconfig.json`](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/tsconfig.json). Applies to the repository TypeScript program (`include: ["**/*"]` with dependencies/build outputs excluded) and checked JavaScript. Nine strict-family defaults were expanded with TypeScript 5.9.3’s own `optionDeclarations` / `getStrictOptionValue`, not assumed from a future documentation version. `strict` is an umbrella entry; its children are distinct settings but not additive independent benefits. These compiler controls are separate from the bundled ESLint helper’s typed/untyped choice.

| Setting / official documentation                                                                                      | Effective value and origin     | Concern               | Independent assessment                                                                                                                                                                           |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------ | --------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| [target](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/target.html)                                                         | `"ESNext"` — explicit          | Runtime compatibility | ESNext exposes current language and library contracts, but those declarations can promise APIs absent from a deployment runtime; Bun compatibility still needs verification.                     |
| [module](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/module.html)                                                         | `"ESNext"` — explicit          | Runtime compatibility | ES module semantics match the authored source and keep dependency syntax predictable, but this is a runtime contract rather than an inherently better module format.                             |
| [alwaysStrict](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/alwaysStrict.html)                                             | `true` — inherited from strict | Runtime semantics     | Strict parsing rejects legacy hazards, but ES modules already execute in strict mode and noEmit means this setting adds no emitted directive here.                                               |
| [noUnusedParameters](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/noUnusedParameters.html)                                 | `true` — explicit              | Maintainability       | An unused parameter can expose missing behavior, but callbacks and interface implementations may intentionally accept arguments they do not use; underscore names are exempt.                    |
| [noPropertyAccessFromIndexSignature](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/noPropertyAccessFromIndexSignature.html) | `true` — explicit              | Readability           | Bracket access distinguishes an index-signature lookup from a declared property, but the notation itself adds no runtime check and can be noisy for intentionally open records.                  |
| [esModuleInterop](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/esModuleInterop.html)                                       | `true` — explicit              | Runtime compatibility | Matches common CommonJS interop patterns and avoids misleading namespace-call assumptions, but synthetic default imports still depend on the actual loader; noEmit generates no helpers here.    |
| [skipLibCheck](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/skipLibCheck.html)                                             | `false` — explicit             | Type safety           | Keeping declaration checks enabled can reveal incompatible dependency contracts, but it increases checking cost and can surface upstream declaration errors outside this repository's ownership. |

### ESLint core lint rules

9 entries. Configuration source: [`eslint.config.ts`](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/eslint.config.ts); rule links point to primary documentation supplied by the locked implementation.

| Rule ID / documentation                                                             | Effective applicability, severity, options | Concern         | Independent assessment                                                                                                                               |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------ | --------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [complexity](https://eslint.org/docs/latest/rules/complexity)                       | [S01](#s01) **warn** [O046](#o046)         | Maintainability | Branch counts flag functions needing review, but a fixed threshold does not distinguish clear dispatch tables from tangled state transitions.        |
| [max-lines](https://eslint.org/docs/latest/rules/max-lines)                         | [S01](#s01) **error** [O053](#o053)        | Maintainability | A size ceiling can prompt cohesive decomposition, but counting comments and blanks can punish documentation and fragment otherwise coherent modules. |
| [no-empty](https://eslint.org/docs/latest/rules/no-empty)                           | [S01](#s01) **error** [O058](#o058)        | Maintainability | Empty blocks can conceal unfinished work or swallowed errors, but intentional no-op branches require context that syntax cannot establish.           |
| [no-inner-declarations](https://eslint.org/docs/latest/rules/no-inner-declarations) | [S01](#s01) **warn** [O062](#o062)         | Maintainability | Restrictions can avoid confusing declaration semantics in nested blocks, but modern block-scoped functions can be a clear local design.              |
| [no-magic-numbers](https://eslint.org/docs/latest/rules/no-magic-numbers)           | [S07](#s07) **warn** [O064](#o064)         | Readability     | Names can reveal units and domain policy, but exempting only 0, 1, and 2 generates noise for obvious local counts and indices.                       |
| [no-shadow](https://eslint.org/docs/latest/rules/no-shadow)                         | [S07](#s07) **warn** [O069](#o069)         | Readability     | Prevents confusing reuse of outer names, but short callbacks can use conventional local names safely without harming review.                         |
| [no-use-before-define](https://eslint.org/docs/latest/rules/no-use-before-define)   | [S07](#s07) **warn** [O076](#o076)         | Readability     | Declaration-first reading can help, but forbidding safe hoisted functions adds reordering work without preventing a runtime defect.                  |
| [no-useless-escape](https://eslint.org/docs/latest/rules/no-useless-escape)         | [S01](#s01) **error** [O077](#o077)        | Readability     | Fewer escapes can clarify patterns and strings, but some redundant escapes deliberately make delimiters or intended punctuation clearer.             |
| [require-yield](https://eslint.org/docs/latest/rules/require-yield)                 | [S01](#s01) **error** [O000](#o000)        | Correctness     | A generator without yields may be accidental, but an intentionally empty iterator can satisfy a legitimate protocol.                                 |

| [@typescript-eslint/no-unnecessary-type-parameters](https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-unnecessary-type-parameters) | [S03](#s03) **error** [O000](#o000)        | Maintainability | Removes generics that express no relationship, but extension points or public inference behavior may justify retaining one.                             |

### @stylistic lint rules

12 entries. Configuration source: [`eslint.config.ts`](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/eslint.config.ts); rule links point to primary documentation supplied by the locked implementation.

| Rule ID / documentation                                                                                  | Effective applicability, severity, options | Concern     | Independent assessment                                                                                                                                        |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------ | ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [@stylistic/jsx-closing-tag-location](https://eslint.style/rules/jsx-closing-tag-location)               | [S01](#s01) **error** [O008](#o008)        | Readability | Aligned closing tags can expose nesting, but this rule's contribution overlaps heavily with Prettier.                                                         |
| [@stylistic/jsx-curly-brace-presence](https://eslint.style/rules/jsx-curly-brace-presence)               | [S01](#s01) **error** [O009](#o009)        | Readability | Removing literal-only expression wrappers simplifies JSX, but uniform expression notation can be useful in generated or frequently edited attributes.         |
| [@stylistic/jsx-function-call-newline](https://eslint.style/rules/jsx-function-call-newline)             | [S01](#s01) **error** [O012](#o012)        | Readability | Separating JSX arguments makes their extent visible, but enforcing it for small arguments adds vertical space without much clarity.                           |
| [@stylistic/jsx-indent-props](https://eslint.style/rules/jsx-indent-props)                               | [S01](#s01) **error** [O013](#o013)        | Readability | Indentation groups attributes beneath their element, but Prettier already provides that structure.                                                            |
| [@stylistic/jsx-max-props-per-line](https://eslint.style/rules/jsx-max-props-per-line)                   | [S01](#s01) **error** [O014](#o014)        | Readability | One attribute per line can ease reviewing long elements, but a strict limit expands small obvious elements unnecessarily.                                     |
| [@stylistic/lines-between-class-members](https://eslint.style/rules/lines-between-class-members)         | [S01](#s01) **error** [O020](#o020)        | Readability | Method boundaries become easier to scan, but mandatory blank lines around every method can expand compact related accessors. Property pairs are unrestricted. |
| [@stylistic/new-parens](https://eslint.style/rules/new-parens)                                           | [S01](#s01) **error** [O003](#o003)        | Readability | Parentheses make constructor invocation explicit, but zero-argument new syntax is unambiguous and already normalized by Prettier.                             |
| [@stylistic/no-extra-parens](https://eslint.style/rules/no-extra-parens)                                 | [S01](#s01) **error** [O022](#o022)        | Readability | Removing redundant parentheses can reduce clutter, but explicit grouping may communicate intent even when precedence does not require it.                     |
| [@stylistic/padding-line-between-statements](https://eslint.style/rules/padding-line-between-statements) | [S01](#s01) **warn** [O030](#o030)         | Readability | Gaps distinguish imports, declarations, control flow, and returns, but mandatory gaps can fragment a short cohesive operation.                                |
| [@stylistic/space-unary-ops](https://eslint.style/rules/space-unary-ops)                                 | [S01](#s01) **error** [O033](#o033)        | Readability | Spacing can distinguish word operators from operands, but most punctuation choices are redundant with the formatter.                                          |
| [@stylistic/spaced-comment](https://eslint.style/rules/spaced-comment)                                   | [S01](#s01) **error** [O034](#o034)        | Readability | A gap after comment markers improves scanning, but decoration and machine-readable directives can need special handling.                                      |
| [@stylistic/wrap-iife](https://eslint.style/rules/wrap-iife)                                             | [S01](#s01) **error** [O036](#o036)        | Readability | Parentheses can mark an immediately executed function, but the exact wrapping style adds little once the construct is recognized.                             |

### unicorn lint rules

142 entries. Configuration source: [`eslint.config.ts`](https://github.com/jdeurt/agents/blob/50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2/eslint.config.ts); rule links point to primary documentation supplied by the locked implementation.

| [unicorn/consistent-json-file-read](https://github.com/sindresorhus/eslint-plugin-unicorn/blob/v78.0.0/docs/rules/consistent-json-file-read.md)                                 | [S01](#s01) **error** [O088](#o088)        | Readability     | A common read/decode pattern makes JSON input boundaries recognizable, but differing runtimes and encodings can justify alternatives.                              |

| [unicorn/logical-assignment-operators](https://github.com/sindresorhus/eslint-plugin-unicorn/blob/v78.0.0/docs/rules/logical-assignment-operators.md)                           | [S01](#s01) **error** [O003](#o003)        | Readability     | Logical assignment can express guarded updates compactly, but expanded conditionals may make effects and absent-value semantics clearer.                           |

| [unicorn/no-async-promise-finally](https://github.com/sindresorhus/eslint-plugin-unicorn/blob/v78.0.0/docs/rules/no-async-promise-finally.md)                                   | [S01](#s01) **error** [O000](#o000)        | Correctness     | Async cleanup delays completion and can replace outcomes, but awaited asynchronous cleanup in finally is often precisely the intended contract.                    |
| [unicorn/no-blob-to-file](https://github.com/sindresorhus/eslint-plugin-unicorn/blob/v78.0.0/docs/rules/no-blob-to-file.md)                                                     | [S01](#s01) **error** [O000](#o000)        | Performance     | Avoids redundant data wrappers when a File can be constructed directly, but an intermediate Blob may have its own contract or reuse.                               |

| [unicorn/no-duplicate-loops](https://github.com/sindresorhus/eslint-plugin-unicorn/blob/v78.0.0/docs/rules/no-duplicate-loops.md)                                               | [S01](#s01) **error** [O000](#o000)        | Performance     | Avoids mapping/filtering just before a traversal, but a declarative transformation can be clearer and intentionally reused.                                        |
| [unicorn/no-empty-file](https://github.com/sindresorhus/eslint-plugin-unicorn/blob/v78.0.0/docs/rules/no-empty-file.md)                                                         | [S01](#s01) **error** [O099](#o099)        | Maintainability | Can identify accidental scaffolding, but empty marker modules and generated entry points may have a real integration role.                                         |
