| [react-doctor/r3f-no-clone-in-use-frame](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-no-clone-in-use-frame)                                                       | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>target: react, r3f; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                            | Performance     | Reusing frame scratch objects can reduce garbage, but occasional cloning may be harmless and safer for ownership.                                           |
| [react-doctor/r3f-no-extend-three-namespace](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-no-extend-three-namespace)                                               | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>target: react, r3f; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                            | Performance     | Registering only needed constructors may improve tree shaking, but dynamic catalogs can legitimately need broader registration.                             |
| [react-doctor/r3f-no-fresh-use-three-selector](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-no-fresh-use-three-selector)                                           | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>target: react, r3f, r3f:6; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                     | Performance     | Stable selected values avoid unnecessary subscriptions and updates, but equality functions and inexpensive derived objects can be valid.                    |
| [react-doctor/r3f-no-inline-resource-prop](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-no-inline-resource-prop)                                                   | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>J; target: react, r3f; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                         | Lifecycle       | Stable resources can avoid allocation and disposal churn, but intentionally replacing a resource when inputs change remains valid.                          |
| [react-doctor/r3f-no-manual-canvas-resize](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-no-manual-canvas-resize)                                                   | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>target: react, r3f; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                            | Ownership       | One resize owner avoids competing dimensions, but specialized rendering targets can require deliberate manual sizing.                                       |
| [react-doctor/r3f-no-new-in-use-frame](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-no-new-in-use-frame)                                                           | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>target: react, r3f; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                            | Performance     | Reusable frame objects can reduce garbage collection, but pooling everything increases mutable state and needs workload evidence.                           |
| [react-doctor/r3f-no-state-in-pointer-move](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-no-state-in-pointer-move)                                                 | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>target: react, r3f; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                            | Performance     | Transient refs can avoid React updates for every pointer sample, but accessible or semantic UI feedback may require state changes.                          |
| [react-doctor/r3f-no-state-in-use-frame](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-no-state-in-use-frame)                                                       | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>target: react, r3f; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                            | Performance     | Direct scene updates avoid reconciling React each frame, but guarded infrequent state transitions inside a frame callback can be correct.                   |
| [react-doctor/r3f-no-sync-readback-in-use-frame](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-no-sync-readback-in-use-frame)                                       | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>target: react, r3f; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                            | Performance     | Async or reduced-frequency readback avoids GPU/CPU stalls, but exact frame-dependent algorithms may require measured synchronous reads.                     |
| [react-doctor/r3f-prefer-gpu-instanced-animation](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-prefer-gpu-instanced-animation)                                     | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>target: react, r3f; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                            | Performance     | GPU animation can reduce repeated CPU transforms, but shader complexity, instance count, and debugging costs determine the tradeoff.                        |
| [react-doctor/r3f-prefer-gpu-position-animation](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-prefer-gpu-position-animation)                                       | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>target: react, r3f; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                            | Performance     | GPU vertex updates can avoid repeated uploads, but CPU simulation may be simpler or required by other consumers.                                            |
| [react-doctor/r3f-prefer-instanced-mesh](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-prefer-instanced-mesh)                                                       | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>J; target: react, r3f; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                         | Performance     | Instancing can reduce draw calls for compatible meshes, but ordering, materials, culling, and interaction contracts may require separate objects.           |
| [react-doctor/r3f-prefer-use-loader](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-prefer-use-loader)                                                               | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>target: react, r3f, r3f:3; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                     | Lifecycle       | Renderer-aware loading can centralize caching and Suspense, but imperative loading can be valid for dynamic ownership or unsupported resource flows.        |
| [react-doctor/r3f-require-dynamic-buffer-usage](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-require-dynamic-buffer-usage)                                         | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>target: react, r3f; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                            | Performance     | A dynamic usage hint may improve frequent uploads, but driver behavior and upload patterns determine whether it matters.                                    |
| [react-doctor/r3f-require-environment-for-metal](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-require-environment-for-metal)                                       | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>J; target: react, r3f; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                         | Rendering       | Environment lighting makes reflective metal visible, but dark or stylized materials can intentionally omit it.                                              |
| [react-doctor/r3f-require-lighting-for-pbr](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-require-lighting-for-pbr)                                                 | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>J; target: react, r3f; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                         | Rendering       | Lighting is needed for ordinary PBR visibility, but intentional darkness or custom emissive behavior can be valid.                                          |
| [react-doctor/r3f-valid-shadow-map-size](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/r3f-valid-shadow-map-size)                                                       | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>J; target: react, r3f; constructs: r3f, webgl                                                                                         | Compatibility   | Valid bounded dimensions prevent unsupported allocations, but power-of-two assumptions may depend on backend and device capabilities.                       |
| [react-doctor/react-router-guard-aborted-handle-error](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/react-router-guard-aborted-handle-error)                           | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>N; target: react-router:7, react-router-framework                                                                                     | Observability   | Filtering expected cancellations reduces misleading errors, but cancellation-associated real failures may still need diagnostic visibility.                 |
| [react-doctor/react-router-internal-route-anchor](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/react-router-internal-route-anchor)                                     | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>N; J; target: react-router:6.4                                                                                                        | Navigation      | Router links can preserve client navigation state, but document navigation may be deliberate for resets, downloads, or server-owned routes.                 |
| [react-doctor/react-router-loader-parallel-fetch](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/react-router-loader-parallel-fetch)                                     | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>N; target: react, react-router:6.4                                                                                                    | Performance     | Parallel independent fetches avoid serial delay, but transactions, rate limits, and partial-failure handling can require sequencing.                        |
| [react-doctor/react-router-no-empty-leaf-route](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/react-router-no-empty-leaf-route)                                         | [S09](#s09) **warn** [O000](#o000)<br>N; target: react-router:6.4                                                                                                           | Correctness     | An empty UI leaf often hides missing content, but a deliberate blank, redirect, or resource route may be valid.                                             |
| [react-doctor/react-router-no-redirect-in-try-catch](https://react.doctor/docs/rules/react-doctor/react-router-no-redirect-in-try-catch)                               | [S09](#s09) **error** [O000](#o000)<br>N; target: react-router:6.4                                                                                                          | Correctness     | Prevents broad catches from swallowing redirect responses, but an explicit catch-and-rethrow policy can legitimately inspect them.                          |

## Convinced it offers little value

**86 active catalog entries.** All judgments in this section are “Convinced it offers little value”.

### Formatting controls

Options are from Prettier 3.9.9’s actual support metadata, combined with root configuration. The command formats owned supported files; this repository excludes all `plugins/` content. A syntax-specific option is dormant when that syntax is absent. Prettier’s intrinsic printer behavior is represented by the `prettier/prettier` policy row, not thousands of inferred micro-rules.

| Option / source documentation                                                                           | Effective value and applicability                                                    | Concern     | Independent assessment                                                                                                                      |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [arrowParens](https://prettier.io/docs/options#arrow-function-parentheses)                              | `"always"` (explicit); JavaScript/TypeScript arrow parameters                        | Consistency | Parentheses around every single arrow parameter are a notation preference; both forms are clear.                                            |
| [bracketSameLine](https://prettier.io/docs/options#bracket-line)                                        | `false` (inherited default); Multiline HTML/JSX/Vue/Angular opening tags             | Consistency | Choosing a separate closing bracket line is layout taste rather than a distinct code-quality safeguard.                                     |
| [bracketSpacing](https://prettier.io/docs/options#bracket-spacing)                                      | `true` (explicit); Supported object/braced constructs                                | Consistency | Spaces inside object braces are a cosmetic choice already made consistent by the formatter.                                                 |
| [experimentalOperatorPosition](https://prettier.io/docs/options#experimental-operator-position)         | `"end"` (inherited default); Wrapped JavaScript/TypeScript binary expressions        | Consistency | Operator-at-line-end versus operator-at-line-start is an equivalent layout convention.                                                      |
| [experimentalTernaries](https://prettier.io/docs/options#experimental-ternaries)                        | `false` (inherited default); JavaScript/TypeScript ternaries                         | Consistency | Keeping the standard ternary layout instead of the experimental layout is a readability preference without a general correctness advantage. |
| [singleQuote](https://prettier.io/docs/options#quotes)                                                  | `false` (explicit); Supported non-JSX quoted strings; escape-count exceptions remain | Consistency | Double versus single JavaScript quotes does not inherently improve code comprehension.                                                      |
| [jsxSingleQuote](https://prettier.io/docs/options#jsx-quotes)                                           | `false` (inherited default); JSX/TSX attribute strings                               | Consistency | Double JSX quotes are familiar but are still a cosmetic preference over equivalent single quotes.                                           |
| [vueIndentScriptAndStyle](https://prettier.io/docs/options#vue-files-script-and-style-tags-indentation) | `false` (inherited default); Vue script/style blocks                                 | Consistency | Whether Vue script/style contents receive an extra indentation level is a presentation preference.                                          |
| [tabWidth](https://prettier.io/docs/options#tab-width)                                                  | `2` (explicit); Indentation and tab display width                                    | Consistency | Two spaces rather than another consistent indent width is a subjective density choice.                                                      |
| [useTabs](https://prettier.io/docs/options#tabs)                                                        | `false` (explicit); All supported printers; alignment spaces remain                  | Consistency | Spaces versus tabs is a team preference; both can represent nesting clearly when tools agree.                                               |

### Deliberate Stylistic/Prettier conflicts

These 12 omissions are explicitly explained in the source. Other off compatibility keys do not necessarily represent actual conflicts. The table reproduces the substance of the source rationale; none was changed.

| Off rule                               | Source reason                                                                          |
| -------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| @stylistic/arrow-parens                | Preset as-needed parentheses conflict with Prettier always.                            |
| @stylistic/brace-style                 | Stroustrup separates else/catch/finally; Prettier keeps them beside the closing brace. |
| @stylistic/comma-dangle                | Preset requires function-list trailing commas; Prettier es5 omits them.                |
| @stylistic/indent                      | Prettier owns nested callback, JSX and ternary indentation.                            |
| @stylistic/indent-binary-ops           | Wrapped concatenation argument indentation differs.                                    |
| @stylistic/member-delimiter-style      | Prettier uses TypeScript member semicolons rather than none/comma.                     |
| @stylistic/operator-linebreak          | Prettier can end a line with assignment; preset before forbids it.                     |
| @stylistic/quote-props                 | Prettier quotes only required keys; preset consistent-as-needed quotes siblings.       |
| @stylistic/quotes                      | Prettier double quotes conflict with preset single quotes.                             |
| @stylistic/semi                        | Prettier semicolons conflict with preset omission.                                     |
| @stylistic/jsx-one-expression-per-line | Prettier preserves inline JSX text/element whitespace.                                 |
| @stylistic/multiline-ternary           | Prettier can wrap one branch while retaining the condition/operator line.              |

## Normalized options registry

Each value is the complete option array **after** severity from ESLint’s merged result. This includes normalized defaults that ESLint materializes. Remaining unmaterialized implementation defaults come from the pinned rule package and were read in its metadata/source. Empty options do not override implementation defaults. Off-rule option tuples are retained too, preventing a disabled rule from being mistaken for an active override.

## Shared settings and path-gate appendix

### JSDoc contexts and settings

The seven JSDoc rules share the following exact settings. Their per-rule contexts and all overload/void/destructuring exceptions are in the options registry. Declaration summaries cover top-level functions/bindings and all classes/members; nested callbacks are not globally required to have docs. JSDoc mode is TypeScript.

```json
{
  "mode": "typescript",
  "contexts": [
    "Program > :matches(FunctionDeclaration, TSDeclareFunction)",
    "Program > :matches(ExportNamedDeclaration, ExportDefaultDeclaration) > :matches(FunctionDeclaration, TSDeclareFunction)",
    "Program > VariableDeclaration > VariableDeclarator > :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression)",
    "Program > VariableDeclaration > VariableDeclarator > :matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion) :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression):not(Program > VariableDeclaration > VariableDeclarator > :matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion) :not(:matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion)) :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression))",
    "Program > ExportNamedDeclaration > VariableDeclaration > VariableDeclarator > :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression)",
    "Program > ExportNamedDeclaration > VariableDeclaration > VariableDeclarator > :matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion) :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression):not(Program > ExportNamedDeclaration > VariableDeclaration > VariableDeclarator > :matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion) :not(:matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion)) :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression))",
    "Program > ExportDefaultDeclaration > :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression)",
    "Program > ExportDefaultDeclaration > :matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion) :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression):not(Program > ExportDefaultDeclaration > :matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion) :not(:matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion)) :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression))",
    "Program > ExpressionStatement > AssignmentExpression > :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression)",
    "Program > ExpressionStatement > AssignmentExpression > :matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion) :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression):not(Program > ExpressionStatement > AssignmentExpression > :matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion) :not(:matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion)) :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression))",
    "Program > TSExportAssignment > :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression)",
    "Program > TSExportAssignment > :matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion) :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression):not(Program > TSExportAssignment > :matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion) :not(:matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion)) :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression))",
    ":matches(MethodDefinition, TSAbstractMethodDefinition) > :matches(FunctionExpression, TSEmptyBodyFunctionExpression)",
    "PropertyDefinition > :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression)",
    "PropertyDefinition > :matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion) > :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression)",
    "PropertyDefinition > :matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion) > :matches(TSSatisfiesExpression, TSAsExpression, TSTypeAssertion) > :matches(ArrowFunctionExpression, FunctionExpression)",
    "ClassDeclaration",
    "ClassExpression"
  ]
}
```

### React Doctor test/tooling gate

For rows marked N, the locked implementation normalizes separators, checks special basenames case-insensitively, checks filename substrings, then checks directory fragments relative to the last source-root marker. Tool/example fragments listed as sourceExceptions are not excluded merely because they occur under a recognized source root. Explicit test/fixture fragments still apply. Dot-directory fragments are checked against the full path. This is implementation behavior, not a blanket assertion that every test file is ignored by ESLint. Other rules still run.

### Written directive coverage and scanner defaults

The numbered guide is covered by exact 1A–9C identifiers. Formatting/Linting precedence and setup paragraphs map to P01–P12; verification maps to P13–P15; declaration/function-placement cautions map to P16–P17. Its remaining policy-details paragraphs map to the named lint, formatter and written-style entries. Code Review maps to R01–R13 in source section order. Repository code/runtime/ownership/check obligations map to G01–G05; its repeated 350-line and unsafe-assertion constraints are represented by 1D, 4D, 4E and the corresponding lint entries. The repository specifically allows Zod Mini or checked control flow for unknown input. Publication mechanics and general documentation organization are outside this code-quality catalog.

R06–R11 preserve configured scanner roots, ignores and thresholds. With no project configuration, the canonical clone fallback is `jscpd@5 . --reporters ai --kind exact,renamed,similar --ignore-identifiers --ignore-literals --ignore-annotations --max-gap-lines 2 --similarity 0.8 --cross-formats js-ts`. The dead-code fallback is `jscpd@5 . --dead-code --dead-code-categories all --min-confidence 0 --include-tests --include-entry-exports --reporters ai`. These defaults are recorded, not treated as proof that scanner findings are defects. The MCP requests all three kinds and checks total/returned/truncated counts; CLI fills gaps and performs dead-code analysis.

### Execution controls outside the formatter style count

| Control           | Default/effective execution behavior                |
| ----------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
| checkIgnorePragma | false                                               |
| cursorOffset      | -1                                                  |
| filepath          | Supplied file path; undefined as API default        |
| insertPragma      | false                                               |
| parser            | Inferred from file path                             |
| plugins           | No global plugin; TOML override is cataloged as F21 |
| rangeEnd          | Infinity (whole file)                               |
| rangeStart        | 0                                                   |
| requirePragma     | false                                               |

The root compiler additionally declares `include: ["**/*"]` and excludes `node_modules`, `dist`, `.marketplace-build`, `.marketplace-verify`; normal compiler supported-file rules still apply. `.prettierignore` excludes `plugins/`, so a clean formatter check does not prove that every authored skill paragraph was formatted. The ESLint `prettier/prettier` rule sets `usePrettierrc:false` and passes the shared options explicitly. The CLI reads the root configuration.

## Documentation read and evidence boundaries

For **every active lint ID**, the locked implementation’s actual description was read where provided; primary rule documentation or implementation supplied the fallback. Available default options, schema and messages were also inspected. All active React Doctor original `recommendation` fields were also read. The full written style guide, adoption/review instructions, compiler option descriptions and Prettier option descriptions were read. Rule documentation links in the inventory come from primary package metadata; custom rules link to their actual implementation. This does **not** claim that every linked website was separately fetched or that every rule was individually executed. Official ESLint import-sort, TypeScript configuration and Prettier option references were consulted; selected implementation checks resolved ambiguous names, no-op settings and runtime gates.

Reproduction evidence was saved during the audit at `/Users/juan/Documents/ChatGPT/DX/reports/agents-code-rules-audit-evidence/`: pre/post merged JSON, metadata, raw React Doctor recommendations, per-rule assessments, exact settings/gates, build/check logs and the removal probe output. That working evidence is not required to use this committed report: all IDs, decisions, applicability variants, explicit options, disabled states and reproduction code are present here. The external directory is not a replacement for this repository artifact.

Limits: classifications are static engineering judgments, not measured false-positive rates or workload benchmarks. Enabled rule counts include dormant framework checks and overlaps; they are not counts of detected defects. `calculateConfigForFile` proves configuration selection without requiring every synthetic path to belong to a real TypeScript program. Actual linting of the repository and focused import probes were run separately. Runtime/package-specific visitors may narrow behavior beyond syntactic applicability. Public documentation can evolve; exact behavior for this audit is pinned by `bun.lock` and the audited source revision. Full suites run in CI only, per the task instruction.

## Validation and coverage checklist

- [x] Repository identity and freshly fetched default-branch baseline verified; isolated branch used.
- [x] All 48 canonical numbered style IDs matched the parsed source heading set exactly.
- [x] All unnumbered quality adoption/review directives and repository code constraints inventoried; unrelated plugin instructions excluded explicitly.
- [x] All 16 mode/extension configurations expanded using ESLint; grouped extensions compared for exact rule equality; CommonJS source-type differences retained.
- [x] Active lint assessment keys equal the complete enabled union: 1,156; no duplicate or unknown keys.
- [x] All 1157 active rule/configuration variants and all off-state variants have exact profile and options references.
- [x] All 363 off-only keys and 22 mixed-state IDs enumerated separately; removed sort-imports excluded from active totals.
- [x] All 20 exposed Prettier style options and TOML override included; nine execution-only controls disclosed.
- [x] All 25 explicit compiler options and nine strict-family settings included; source coverage paths recorded.
- [x] All active entries have exactly one of the three classifications, a source/documentation link and applicability; quality/uncertain rows have specific assessments.
- [x] Pre/post rule dictionaries compare equal in all 16 profiles after deleting only sort-imports from the baseline.
- [x] Focused ESLint flow probes confirm import declaration/member sorting disappears while duplicate-import and post-import-spacing diagnostics remain, with unrelated diagnostics unchanged.
- [x] Lint, typecheck, format check, imported-source validation, build, generated consistency check and git diff whitespace check passed for the removal; final report formatting/completeness checked separately.
- [x] Generated Claude/Codex policy copies are byte-identical to root files; no generated output committed and no installed cache edited.
- [x] No local unit tests or full suite run; repository CI owns the full suite.

- [ ] Independent review pending: the review coordinator will arrange it after receiving this exact commit/report. No reviewer was started.

## Reproduce the configuration inventory

From a clean checkout of the commit containing this report, use Bun 1.4.3 and run `bun install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts`. Save the following temporary script in the repository root as `audit-expand.tmp.ts`, run `bun audit-expand.tmp.ts > /tmp/agents-rules.json`, then remove the temporary script. It prints all 16 full configurations and original rule metadata; use the emitted JSON to independently compare the report’s active IDs, severities, options and file sets. To reproduce the baseline, run the same script in a separate checkout of `50afb8a256431fce31acd2617590925c298845d2`. Do not reset a dirty checkout.

```ts
import { ESLint } from "eslint";
import { builtinRules } from "eslint/use-at-your-own-risk";
import { createConfig } from "./eslint.config.ts";

const results = {};
const metadata = {};
for (const typed of [true, false]) {
  const eslint = new ESLint({
    cwd: process.cwd(),
    overrideConfigFile: true,
    overrideConfig: createConfig({ typed, tsconfigRootDir: process.cwd() }),
  });
  for (const ext of ["js", "mjs", "cjs", "jsx", "ts", "mts", "cts", "tsx"]) {
    const config = await eslint.calculateConfigForFile(`audit-probe.${ext}`);
    results[`${typed ? "T" : "U"}-${ext}`] = {
      rules: config.rules,
      settings: config.settings,
      sourceType: config.languageOptions.sourceType,
      parserOptions: config.languageOptions.parserOptions,
    };
    for (const id of Object.keys(config.rules)) {
      const split = id.lastIndexOf("/");
      const rule = split < 0 ? builtinRules.get(id) : config.plugins[id.slice(0, split)]?.rules?.[id.slice(split + 1)];
      metadata[id] = rule?.meta;
    }
  }
}
console.log(JSON.stringify({ results, metadata }, null, 2));
```

Count active rules using numeric severity greater than zero. Preserve rule IDs across families; never deduplicate by description. Compare each complete option array per profile, not only severity. For raw React Doctor recommendations, inspect `oxlint-plugin-react-doctor`’s exported rule objects; for formatter defaults use `prettier.getSupportInfo()` with the TOML plugin; for strict compiler defaults use TypeScript 5.9.3’s `optionDeclarations.filter(o => o.strictFlag)` and `getStrictOptionValue(parsedOptions, name)`.
